— i.e. **the mechanism inventing caller data from a callable's internals**, which is the **same false-green class as reading an inherited prototype member as a value** (the hazard `§2.5` item 2 closes on the other side: *"a value the caller never supplied"*). Clause (i)'s list enumerates `null`/`undefined`/a string/a number/an array; **the function belongs to the same class by the clause's own INTENT** — *"a `values` map is a plain record of caller data, never a callable whose incidental own properties are a data map"* — so this ruling **completes** clause (i) rather than contradicting it. **No `malformed-spec`-style reason is invented for `values`** (it is caller data; prohibition 3), and no shape is added to the skip vocabulary. **(c) THE EXACT EXPECTED OUTCOME, for the drive a row must assert:** `const fn = function f(a) {}` ⇒ `project(fn, specOf)` yields, for **every** spec entry, **`missing-value`** — including **a spec key literally named `'length'`, `'name'` or `'prototype'`** (each is a real own property of `fn`, and **each is still skipped `missing-value`**: `fn` is not a record, so it holds **no own keys for this contract's purposes**), `applied` is `{}`, `ok`-irrelevant (no applier call), and **nothing throws** (`I-7`). **A row asserting `applied['--n'] === '1'` for the `'length'` drive FAILS — that reading is the module's current behaviour and NOT this contract's.** **(d) THE RULE FOR THE OTHER POSITIONS WHERE `isObjectLike` ACCEPTS CALLABLES — stated explicitly so a TestWriter does NOT over-generalise the `values` ruling into them.** **The `values` position is the ONLY position with this restriction, and the other positions are DELIBERATELY duck-typed:** **(1) the SINK (`applyProjection`'s second argument)** — a FUNCTION is still object-like: `sink['style']` and `style['setProperty']` are read and **the callability of `setProperty` is the whole test**; a function carrying a callable `setProperty` on its own `style` is a **USABLE sink** and the write proceeds, while a function carrying no such path is **`sink-unusable`** for **every** key (`F-6`, `§2.3` item 4). **`isObjectLike`'s callable allowance is REQUIRED here** — the caller may pass any object at all, and the sink is judged **only** by whether the one method the contract needs is callable. **(2) the `applied` field of a hand-built projection** — a function as `projection.applied` is object-like: its **own enumerable keys** become the planned writes and a **non-primitive** value is `write-refused` (`F-10`); **the applier does not re-validate its input** (`M-13`), so a function there is read as a record **by the same own-key rule** — **the duck-typing is intended and totality HOLDS** (no throw, a decision per key, `F-9`/`F-10`). **(3) the PROJECTION argument itself** (`applyProjection`'s first argument) — a FUNCTION is object-like and **not** a non-record: `projection['applied']` and `projection['skipped']` are read as **own or inherited properties of it**, so `M-11`'s total no-op applies **only** to the true non-records (`null`, `undefined`, a string, a number). **Totality holds in all three: the ruling changes the `values` position and NOTHING else, and a TestWriter must not extend it.** |
   **(v) THE PRESENCE-TEST THROW — THE TRIGGER CLAUSE WIDENED, AND THE HOSTILE-`specOf` CLAUSE, BOTH ADDED 2026-09-27. *(PLACEMENT, recorded so it is not read as drift: this clause belongs to item 3's trigger material and is FILED at the end of the item-3 block — after clauses (i)–(iii), and BEFORE the numbered items 4/5/6 and the later-appended clause (iv) block, which are kept exactly where they were; the item-3 block's own tail is a >2000-character cell that a bounded anchored `edit` cannot safely split, so appending at this boundary is the safe form. It cites item 3's clauses by name, no item or clause is renumbered, and `§2.4`'s item sequence 1–6 is unchanged.)* (the `U-PROJ` FINAL SPEC PASS; `F-02`/`F-03` plus the gate-5 addendum's `PJ-R-06` and its ambiguities `O-10`; `CONTRACT-AMENDED`, and the as-written trigger wording in clauses (a)–(d) above is KEPT VISIBLE and is `SUPERSEDED` only on the ONE shape named in (A) below).** **WHY IT IS NEEDED: the trigger clause above authorised `'accessor-threw'` for ONE shape only — *"the entry's value is a PRESENT OWN PROPERTY OF `values` and the ACT OF READING IT THREW"* — and clause (b) pinned an ABSENT key as `missing-value`; NEITHER clause covered *"the own-property QUESTION ITSELF threw"*, so the gate-5 addendum measured two different reasons for that input (`PJ-R-06` FAIL, ambiguity `O-10`). TWO clauses now close it, and BOTH are stated in the words a row can assert.** **(A) A `values` WHOSE OWN-PROPERTY PRESENCE TEST THROWS RECORDS `'accessor-threw'` — THE RULING.** *The reason is `'accessor-threw'` when the module cannot learn whether the entry's key is an own property of `values` because the QUESTION threw (a revoked `Proxy`, a `Proxy` with a throwing `has` trap or a throwing `getOwnPropertyDescriptor` trap, an object whose own `hasOwnProperty` access throws).* **THE REASONING, recorded because the reason is a judgment and not a restatement of the trigger text:** the module **asked the caller's `values` a question about an OWN PROPERTY**, and **the caller's object ANSWERED WITH A THROW** — that is the **same CALLER-DATA layer the eighth reason names** (the layer split is stated at `§3.2`'s `SKIP-THREW-1` cell: a `specOf` entry's own read throwing is the SPEC-MAP layer ⇒ `malformed-spec` with `name: ''`; a `values` read failing is the CALLER-DATA layer ⇒ `accessor-threw` with the entry's own emitted `name`), and here the key **IS named** (the entry carries its own usable `name`), so the decision is a **named caller-data read failure** — *"we could not obtain this caller's value"* — and **not** *"this caller owns no such key"*. **`missing-value` is therefore NOT the reason for this input**, and the two facts stay distinguishable to the consumer, which is the whole point of the eighth member (`§0A` note 2: reusing another reason **silently conflates** facts the consumer cannot recover from apart). **THE OUTCOME, exactly: `project` returns a `Projection` and NEVER throws (`§2.4` item 2, `I-7`); each spec entry still produces EXACTLY ONE decision (`§2.3` item 1, `I-1`); the throwing key's skip carries the entry's own emitted `name` and reason `'accessor-threw'`; the read is not attempted — or, if attempted, its outcome is immaterial, because the question already threw; the OTHER keys are still decided normally; `applied` is an empty null-prototype record when no key is appliable; and NO ninth reason is invented (`§4.4 S-9`).** **THE EXACT REVOKED-PROXY CASES, stated so a row has no reading to choose between:** for `Proxy.revocable({k1:1,k2:2}, {})` + `revoke()` and a `specOf` of `{k1:{name:'--k1',unit:'px'}, k2:{name:'--k2',unit:'px'}}`, the result is **`applied === {}` and `skipped === [{name:'--k1',reason:'accessor-threw'},{name:'--k2',reason:'accessor-threw'}]` in spec-entry order** — **two decisions, both `'accessor-threw'`, named, in-domain, nothing thrown**; and for a single-entry spec the same drive yields **`skipped === [{name:'--k1',reason:'accessor-threw'}]`**. **A `values` whose presence TEST is answered (a healthy `Proxy`, an ordinary object) is INDISTINGUISHABLE from the equivalent plain object and is applied normally — the ruling changes NOTHING for an answered question.** **(B) A TRUE NON-RECORD `values` STAYS `missing-value` — the same input class as clause (i), never a different reason.** A `null`, `undefined`, a string, a number, an array **or a FUNCTION** (clause (iii)'s `FUNCTION-VALUES-1`) contributes **NO own keys**, so every otherwise-well-formed entry is `missing-value`: **there is no own-property question to ask and no throw to catch — that is a DIFFERENT INPUT, not a different reason for the same input.** **A row that drives a non-record `values` and expects `'accessor-threw'` FAILS this item; a row that drives a throwing presence test and expects `missing-value` FAILS it too.** **(C) THE THROWING-`ownKeys` CASE, pinned so it is neither an eighth reason nor a `missing-value`:** when the **entry ENUMERATION** cannot be answered — a `Proxy` whose **`ownKeys` trap throws** — the spec set yields **NO entries at all**: `project` returns **`{applied: {}, skipped: []}`** and **decides nothing** (the same shape and the same reading as clause (i)'s **non-record `specOf`**), because the module's own key enumeration is guarded and **a spec it cannot enumerate contributes no entries** — **no reason is invented, no throw escapes, and the `accessor-threw` reason is NOT used** (that reason is about a KEY's value, and here no key was ever enumerated). **THE REASON DOMAIN IS UNCHANGED: the eight members of `§2.1`'s `ProjectionSkipReason` union, and nothing else.** **(D) THE HOSTILE-`specOf` CLAUSE, PINNED (the shape that had NO clause — the gate-5 addendum's `F-03` class, ambiguity `O-10`'s sibling):** ***A spec set the module CANNOT ENUMERATE contributes NO entries.*** *Concretely: `specOf` is tested with `isObjectLike` (a non-record `specOf` ⇒ zero entries), and its own-key enumeration is taken inside a totality boundary — so a `specOf` whose own-key enumeration CANNOT be answered (a revoked `Proxy`, a `Proxy` with a throwing `ownKeys` trap, an object whose own-key enumeration throws) yields the SAME result as the non-record case: **`Projection{ applied: {}, skipped: [] }`, both records empty, NO decision taken, NOTHING thrown**, and **no new skip reason** — there is no key to name and therefore no skip entry to carry (`§4.4 S-9`: a ninth reason is forbidden; `§2.1`'s eight members are a CLOSED union).* **The deciding clauses, cited so this is the contract's own reading rather than an addition: `§2.4` item 2 (totality — *"for any `values` and any `specOf` … returns a `Projection` and never throws"*), `§3.2 F-11` (a non-record `specOf` ⇒ zero spec entries and both records empty — the same result, reached by the same rule), `§2.3` item 1 (a decision is taken **per ENUMERATED spec entry**, so a set that yields no entries yields no decisions) and `I-1`/`I-7`. **A `specOf` whose ENUMERATION succeeds but whose ENTRY read throws is a DIFFERENT shape and is already pinned at `§3.2`'s `SKIP-THREW-1` cell (`malformed-spec` with `name: ''`) — the two must not be merged: an entry that cannot be READ yields a named skip; a key set that cannot be ENUMERATED yields no entries at all.** **(E) THE `values`-SIDE ENUMERATION TRAP IS THE OTHER DIRECTION, and the clause is stated so the two sides are not merged:** *`values` is NEVER ENUMERATED — the module enumerates the `SPEC` SET (whose own keys are the lookup keys) and asks `values` only an OWN-PROPERTY question per entry. Therefore a `values` whose own-key enumeration would throw (a `Proxy` with a throwing `ownKeys` trap) changes NOTHING: the entries are decided from the own-property question alone and, where it is answered, the values ARE APPLIED.* **This is the gate-5 addendum's `PJ-R-03` green, and it is consistent with clause (D) because (D) is about the `specOf` side: a set the module must ENUMERATE and cannot ⇒ no entries; a `values` it never enumerates ⇒ no effect.** **A LANDED ROW COVERS THIS SHAPE (`tests/layout-projection.test.ts`'s `PRE-4` instrument row asserts the guard is intentional; cited by row id, never by line) and the gate-5 addendum drove it green (`PJ-R-03`: a `Proxy` whose `ownKeys` trap throws ⇒ no throw, the entries **applied** because the own-property test does not consult `ownKeys`; and the `values`-side enumeration of the `specOf` map is the clause-(D) shape when the trap throws on that object).** **`§4.4 S-9` remains the stop condition for a module, a row or a consumer that propagates the throw or invents a ninth reason.**

4. **Number formatting is the applier's own arithmetic, not a delegated dependency.** With
   `format: 'unit'` (or omitted): `${value}${spec.unit}`; with `format: 'number'`:
   `String(value)`. **`0` is a legitimate value and is applied** — a row pins that `0` is not
   mistaken for absent (`F-3`'s mirror case). **The output is a string produced by the JS number →
   string conversion, and this spec does NOT claim a CSS serialization** (Layer declaration anchor 2).
5. **`duplicate-name` is resolved FIRST-WINS.** If two specs produce the same `name`, the **first**
   (in spec order) is applied and the **second** is skipped with `duplicate-name`; the `applied`
   record therefore contains each name **at most once** (`F-2`).
6. **The census half is ABSENT, structurally.** `project`'s parameters are `(values, specOf)` —
   there is **no** `census`, `zones`, `sizes` or `revealed` parameter, and **no** key-set rule that
   consults one. A static row asserts the module imports nothing from a zones/census module
   (ruling 5), and a signature row pins the parameter list (`A-15`).

   **(iv) THE DUPLICATE-DETECTION SET IS WRITTEN AT THE ACCEPTANCE POINT ONLY (`SKIP-ACCEPT-1`), ADDED 2026-09-27 (the ADVERSARIAL-DISPOSITION + GATE-5 DOC-RECONCILIATION pass; finding `ADV-PJ-14` — `CONTRACT-AMENDED`).** **THE RULE, verbatim:** *a name enters the duplicate-detection set AT THE ACCEPTANCE POINT — when the entry is a USABLE `VarSpec` and its name is not already in the set. An entry that is refused BEFORE that point (a `malformed-spec` entry, and an entry whose own read threw) writes NOTHING into the set and therefore reserves NOTHING.* **WHY IT NEEDS PINNING, recorded so the gap is auditable:** the opposite reading is **equally satisfiable by every clause as written** before this pin — if a malformed entry claimed its declared name, then a **later VALID entry carrying that same name would be `duplicate-name`** (clause (ii) above would still hold, since a name collision did occur), and **no `§3` row drove the shape**. **THE DECIDING PRECEDENT, cited rather than invented:** the `U-LISTHOST` adversarial pass's **`ADV-LH-4`** and the node-rule case **`N-5`** (`docs/specs/listhost.md`, cited by section) rule exactly this shape on the sibling mechanism — *"a key becomes 'seen' only when an occurrence is ACCEPTED"*, and **a refused first occurrence reserves nothing**; `§2.1`'s ACCEPTANCE rule there is the same rule this clause pins here. **A second, independent reason this spec's own majority reading is the acceptance point:** the set is written **after** the `malformed-spec`/`duplicate-name` decisions (`§2.4` item 3's fixed precedence) exactly as the refusal is decided **before** the name collision test — and clause (ii)'s *"first-wins means the first DECISION wins"* must not be read as *"a decision that produced NO usable name still wins the name"*. **THE EXACT OUTCOMES, three drives a row must assert:** **(A)** `project({a: 1, b: 2}, {a: {name: '--dup'}, b: {name: '--dup', unit: ''}})` ⇒ `a` is `malformed-spec` and **reserves nothing**, so `b` is **APPLIED** (`applied['--dup'] === '2'`) and **no `duplicate-name` entry exists**; **(B)** the same with **two VALID** entries naming `'--dup'` ⇒ the second IS `duplicate-name` (`F-2`, unchanged) — so the row has a non-vacuous control that the set is **still written** for accepted entries; **(C)** the same with the FIRST entry's own read throwing (`SKIP-THREW-1`) ⇒ the throwing entry is `malformed-spec` with `name: ''` and **reserves nothing**, so the second valid entry naming the same string is **APPLIED**. **A module that writes the set BEFORE the malformed/duplicate decisions FAILS (A) and (C); a module that never writes it FAILS (B).** **THE CLAUSE PAIR THIS INTERACTS WITH, cited as this item requires: `§2.4` item 3 clause (ii) (name collision, never application) and `§2.4` item 5 (first-wins)** — **this clause SCOPES both: the collision is decided against the set, and the set holds only ACCEPTED names.** **`P-PJ-IM-1`'s `23`-class table is NOT re-pinned by this clause** (its class `(10)` drives a duplicate pair whose first member HAS a value, and its cell says so; the new drives land on `F-2` and `F-1`). | **`M-13`'s TRUST half and `F-10`'s COERCION half are two
different statements about two different objects, and BOTH bind:**
**(a) `M-13`'s "it does not re-validate, re-format or re-derive it" means the applier never asks
`project` again** — it does **not** re-run the validity rule of item 3 over the projection's entries,
does **not** consult `values`/`specOf`, does **not** re-derive a skip reason from a spec entry, and
never re-formats an `applied` value against a `VarSpec` (there is no `VarSpec` in its arguments — the
signature is `applyProjection(projection, sink)`); it applies the record it is given.
**(b) `F-10` is the ONE exception, and it is not re-validation of the caller's SPEC: it is the
type-safety coercion of an `applied` VALUE** — a real `CSSStyleDeclaration.setProperty` must never
receive a non-string, so the applier performs `String(value)` **only for a PRIMITIVE**, and **skips a
non-primitive with `write-refused`**. **The coercion does NOT touch the caller's projection** (it
produces a fresh string handed to the sink; `I-14` is unbroken — see `F-15`).
**(c) The two halves therefore never collide: `M-13`'s observable is *"no `project` call, no
spec-based re-derivation"*, `F-10`'s observable is *"the exact string the sink received"*, and the
`§3.2` rows carry them.** **This is a `CONTRACT-AMENDED` ruling in `M-13`'s cell and `F-10`'s cell
(both keep their as-written words and the amendment is dated and named there).**

### 2.5 The record-building rule — `Object.create(null)`, stated falsifiably (2026-09-27 ruling note 3)

**Why this section exists, in one sentence:** a caller-supplied `VarSpec.name` is **any string**
(the contract requires **no** CSS-legal spelling and the unit may **not** invent a name-legality rule),
so `'__proto__'`, `'constructor'`, `'prototype'`, `'toString'` and `'hasOwnProperty'` are **reachable
both as keys of the returned `applied` record and as keys of any internal per-key map** — and on a
plain-object literal the assignment `applied['__proto__'] = 'x'` **silently changes the record's
prototype instead of creating an own property**, dropping the key from `Object.keys` and leaving the
projection **wrong for that key while claiming no skip**. **The full analysis, the four reasons and the
rejected alternatives are §0A's ruling note 3** (they are recorded there and not duplicated here).
**This section states the resulting RULES, which are what a row drives.**

1. **The returned `applied` records are built on `Object.create(null)`** — `Projection.applied` and
   `ApplyResult.applied` **alike** — so **every** key is an **own** property for **every** caller name,
   including `'__proto__'`. **`I-12`** pins the own-key requirement; **`I-13`** pins the null
   prototype. **A row is one call:** a spec naming `'__proto__'` yields an **own key `'__proto__'`**,
   it **appears in `Object.keys(applied)` in spec order**, and **`I-1`'s partition still holds** —
   which is exactly the falsification of the plain-object hazard (`F-13`). **A projection's own
   prototype must be unchanged by any caller-supplied name**: `Object.getPrototypeOf(applied) === null`
   after such a call, and the **module's** prototype chain is never written.
2. **Every internal key→value map is built the same way, and every lookup over caller data is an OWN
   property lookup.** The named cases, each a row's drive:
   - **the duplicate-name detection set** — read with own-property semantics, so a **name colliding
     with an `Object.prototype` member** (`'constructor'`, `'toString'`, `'hasOwnProperty'`,
     `'valueOf'`) is **NOT** misdiagnosed as `duplicate-name` and is **not** treated as already seen
     (`F-2`'s rows drive `'--dup'`; this clause adds the prototype-shaped names to that drive);
     **⟶ WRITE POINT PINNED 2026-09-27 (the ADVERSARIAL-DISPOSITION + GATE-5 DOC-RECONCILIATION
     pass; finding `ADV-PJ-14`, `§2.4` item 3's clause (iv) `SKIP-ACCEPT-1`, the `U-LISTHOST`
     `ADV-LH-4`/`N-5` precedent; the bullet above is KEPT and NOTHING is withdrawn): the set is
     WRITTEN at the ACCEPTANCE POINT ONLY** — a usable `VarSpec` whose name is not already in the set
     — so **a malformed entry (or one whose own read threw) reserves NOTHING** and a later VALID
     entry carrying that same name is **APPLIED**, not `duplicate-name`. **`F-2`'s scoping clause and
     `F-1`'s `SKIP-NAME-1` (2) state the same rule on the rows; `§2.4` item 3 clause (iv) is the
     normative clause.**
   - **the `values` lookup** — an **own-property** read (`Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call`-style,
     or a null-prototype-safe equivalent), so a **plain-object `values`** with **no** own key named
     `'constructor'` yields **`missing-value`**, never the **inherited function**
     `Object.prototype.constructor` (which would be a value **the caller never supplied**).
     **⟶ KEY IDENTIFIED 2026-09-27 (the `U-PROJ` RED-SET REPAIR pass — THIS BULLET named the READ but
     not the KEY, which is the gap `§2.4` item 3's added clause now closes; the bullet above is kept
     and nothing in it is withdrawn): the own property read here is read UNDER THE `specOf` MAP'S OWN
     KEY** — the own-property test is `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(values, <the specOf key>)`
     — **never under the `VarSpec.name`**, which is only the EMITTED name (`applied`'s key, the skip
     entry's `name`). **The two-object rule and its deciding clauses are `§2.4` item 3's added clause
     (1)/(2); this bullet is where it is applied to the `values` read.** **The prototype-shaped case
     named here is therefore the case where the LOOKUP KEY is prototype-shaped** (a spec entry keyed
     `'constructor'`/`'toString'`/`'hasOwnProperty'`/`'valueOf'`/`'prototype'`/`'__proto__'`); a
     prototype-shaped `VarSpec.name` changes **nothing** about the lookup — it is emitted verbatim
     (`M-18`/`M-20`, `I-12`);
   - **the `specOf` entry lookup** — the same own-property semantics, so a spec set that legitimately
     carries an own key `'__proto__'` (e.g. a `Object.create(null)` spec map, or one built with
     `Object.defineProperty`) is **read as its own entry** rather than silently resolving to the
     prototype;
   - **any other map** the implementation keeps (a formatter memo, an order list) — the same rule, and
     **`A-14`'s static sweep is where a new one would be noticed**.
   **`values` and `specOf` are NOT required to be null-prototype objects** — they are the **caller's**
   data, and this unit may not impose a shape on them (ruling note 3, reason 3). **What is pinned is
   the MODULE's own read semantics**: own properties only.
3. **`Projection.skipped` and `ApplyResult.skipped` are ARRAYS of `{name, reason}` records, so no
   key-map hazard exists there, and the null-prototype rule does NOT extend to them.** `name` is a
   **value** (`ProjectionSkip.name`), never an object key; the array's indices are engine-owned
   integer slots. **A later pass that "completes" this ruling by rewriting `skipped` would be changing
   a part of the shape `A-3` never implicated** (ruling note 3, consequence 6).
4. **The copy hazard is a CONSUMER obligation, stated because the ruling exposes it:** the null
   prototype is **destroyed by a naive copy** — **and the correction below states, per method, which
   copy also destroys the KEY.** **⟶ CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (the ADVERSARIAL-DISPOSITION + GATE-5
   DOC-RECONCILIATION pass; the gate-5 blind pass's FAIL `PJ-G-58`, `docs/specs/projection-greens.md`
   §7.1, MEASURED — DOC DRIFT, not a module defect. The as-written clause is KEPT VISIBLE here and
   its CONCLUSION holds; only its per-method MECHANISM was wrong):** *as written — "`{...applied}` and
   `Object.assign({}, applied)` produce a **plain-prototype** object and **re-invoke
   `Object.prototype`'s `__proto__` accessor on the target** when the source holds an own
   `'__proto__'`".* **THE CORRECTED CLAIM, per method, in the words a row can assert:**
   - **`{...applied}` (object spread): the null prototype is DESTROYED and the own `'__proto__'` KEY
     IS KEPT.** Spread is a **DEFINING** copy (`CreateDataPropertyOrThrow` per own enumerable key), so
     **no target setter is invoked** and the target carries an **own** `'__proto__'` data property
     (`spreadKeys === ['__proto__']`, `spreadHasOwn === true`, `spreadRead === '1'`) while its
     prototype is plain. **The original sentence's "re-invoke the accessor" half is FALSE for spread.**
   - **`Object.assign({}, applied)`: BOTH are destroyed — the prototype AND the key.** `Object.assign`
     is an **ASSIGNING** copy (`[[Set]]`), so the copy reaches `Object.prototype`'s `__proto__`
     accessor on the plain target, which **sets the prototype** and creates **no own property**
     (`assignKeys === []`, `assignHasOwn === false`, `assignProto === 'object'`). **Both halves of the
     original sentence HOLD for this method.**
   - **The mandated recipe is unchanged and still required: a consumer copying the record MUST use a
     NULL-PROTOTYPE target** (`Object.assign(Object.create(null), applied)` — or
     `Object.entries`/`Object.fromEntries` + an explicit null-prototype build), which **keeps the own
     key** (`nullTargetKeys === ['__proto__']`, value `'1'`). **A consumer that must preserve the own
     `'__proto__'` key must NOT use `Object.assign({}, …)`; spread preserves the key but not the
     prototype, so NEITHER plain-target form restores the null prototype.** **The module returns a
     record whose keys survive and may not police how a caller copies it** — a contract obligation, not
     a defect. **`§0A` note 3 consequence 3 states the same per-method rule; the two sites agree.**
5. **`readonly` is TYPE-LEVEL and freezes NOTHING at runtime.** `Object.freeze` on the returned record
   is **permitted** (it changes no row — the return value is treated as a value) but **is NOT a
   contract clause**, and **a caller may rely on neither freezing nor non-freezing**. **What IS pinned
   about mutation is provable and directional:** the applier **never** mutates the projection
   (`I-14`), and no returned record **aliases** module-internal state (`I-5`/`I-8`) — so a caller
   mutating its own copy mutates **its own copy of a value**.

   **⟶ PINNED 2026-09-27 (the `§7a` RULING pass, `§7a.1` item 8 — shallow vs deep, and WHO freezes).**
   **Two statements, each with its own subject, and neither may be read as the other:**
   **(i) The ROW's drive has the HARNESS freeze the caller's objects, SHALLOWLY.** The `F-15` drive
   freezes **`p.applied` (the record) and `p` (the projection object)** — **one level, `Object.freeze`
   each, and NOTHING deeper** — before `applyProjection(p, sink)`, and **the module must not throw**:
   reading a frozen record and a frozen projection is legal, and the applier **writes nothing into
   either** (`I-14`). **The `skipped` ARRAY is NOT frozen by this drive** (a shallow freeze of `p`
   does not freeze `p.skipped`), and **a deep freeze is NOT a contract clause** — a later pass wanting
   one is proposing a **different contract** needing its own gate.
   **(ii) The MODULE may freeze its own return, and the caller may rely on NEITHER.** `Object.freeze`
   on a returned `applied` record or on a returned `Projection` is **permitted, changes no row, and is
   not required**: **no row asserts that a returned record IS frozen, and none asserts that it is
   not.** **What a row DOES assert is (a) that the applier produces correct results when its INPUT is
   frozen, and (b) that the projection is observably unchanged after the call** (`F-15`). **Neither
   half is a `CONTRACT-AMENDED` change: the as-written sentences above are kept verbatim and this is
   the reading they already carry** — `F-15`'s cell is annotated with the same two statements.
6. **The APPLIER needs NO guard against a dangerous NAME, and the two halves differ — stated
   explicitly, because the contrast is the point.** The applier passes the name as a **string argument
   to `CSSStyleDeclaration.setProperty(name, value)`**, where `'__proto__'` is an **ordinary string in
   a CSS property-name position**: the DOM API performs **no object-key assignment**, so there is **no
   prototype-write hazard on that side at all**. **The record-key hazard (a JS object keyed by caller
   data) and the CSS-property-name position (a string argument to a DOM API) are different objects** —
   the first is solved by the null prototype, the second needs nothing — and **the applier must NOT
   refuse, sanitize or re-name a caller-supplied name on either half** (prohibition 3). `M-20` drives
   the contrast on a fake sink that records its exact arguments.

## 3. Behaviour (every state / fail-state)

**Layer labels:** **[T]** harness-side · **[H]** host-side · **[U]** real-DOM `ui` leg. Every row is
a **contract row** for the TestWriter; **none is a measurement this pass took.**

### 3.1 Valid / happy states

| id | State | Trigger (exact) | Required behaviour | Layer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **M-1** | **The unit-family shape: one spec, one value, one write** | `project({w: 320}, {w: {name: '--app-width', unit: 'px'}})` then `applyProjection(p, fakeSink)` | `p.applied` is `{'--app-width': '320px'}`; `p.skipped` is `[]`; the sink received **exactly one** `setProperty('--app-width','320px')`; `r.applied` deep-equals `p.applied`; `r.ok === true` | `[T]` |
| **M-1 — CLARIFICATION (2026-09-27, the `U-PROJ` RED-SET REPAIR pass; the row above is KEPT and its assertion is UNCHANGED — this is NOT a `CONTRACT-AMENDED` cell): the KEY this drive already implies is pinned in words.** The lookup key is **the `specOf` map's own key `'w'`**, read as an **own property of `values`** (`{w: 320}`), and the `name` `'--app-width'` is **only the emitted key** of `applied` — which is why this row's `name` is absent from its `values` and its values key is absent from its `applied` (`§2.4` item 3's key clause (1)). **A row that reads `values[name]` here would make THIS row `missing-value` and therefore cannot be `M-1`** — the contradiction is the rule's proof, and no assertion above changes. | `[T]` |

| **M-4** | **An empty `unit` is valid and means "no unit"** | `{name:'--z', unit:''}`, value `7` | `applied` is `{'--z': '7'}`; **`unit` was not defaulted to any literal** | `[T]` |

The two observables differ, and a row that asserts one for the other shape FAILS.** | `[T]` |
| **M-10** | **`projectVar` agrees with `project` on the same input** | `projectVar(spec, value)` vs `project(values, {k: spec})` | for every §3 row's inputs, the single-key result's `written` equals `project`'s `applied[k]` **and** its `skip` is deep-equal to the corresponding entry in `skipped` (or both `null`/absent) — **one authority, two entry points** **⟶ REACH PINNED 2026-09-27 (the ADVERSARIAL-DISPOSITION + GATE-5 DOC-RECONCILIATION pass; the gate-5 blind pass's recorded ambiguity `O-2`, `§3b`-3; the clause above is KEPT and is given its domain rather than rewritten).** **`projectVar(spec, value)` is handed an ALREADY-OBTAINED value, so *"for every §3 row's inputs"* is SCOPED TO THE INPUTS `projectVar` CAN EXPRESS: a spec plus an OBTAINED VALUE.** **The two read-dependent shapes are OUTSIDE this row's domain and are covered by `project` alone (no row may assert them through `projectVar`):** **"the key is ABSENT from `values`" (`F-3` — an obtained `undefined` means *a present `undefined`* ⇒ `not-a-number`) and "the READ threw" (`F-4B` — there is no read at this entry point).** **A channel for either would be a NEW CONTRACT needing its own gate (a third argument, a sentinel or a result field — `S-9`/`S-11`'s class), so none is invented here.** **What this row DOES assert, unchanged: the two fields are EQUIVALENT and EXHAUSTIVE on every expressible drive — `written === null` ⟺ `skip !== null`, `written` agree with `project`'s `applied` and `skip` deep-equal its skip entry — including the `unit: ''` drive where `written` is a short but REAL string (never a truthiness test).** | `[T]` |

| **M-12** | **A projection with an empty `applied` applies nothing** | `project({}, {})`; then apply | `applied` `{}`; `skipped` `[]`; the sink received **zero** calls; `ok === true`; **nothing throws** | `[T]` |
| **M-13** | **A hand-built projection object is honoured** | a literal `{applied: {…}, skipped: []}` passed straight to `applyProjection` | the applier writes exactly that record — **it does not re-validate, re-format or re-derive it**. *(A contract decision: the applier trusts the projection it is given; §7 item 7.)* **⟶ `CONTRACT-AMENDED` 2026-09-27 (the `§7a` RULING pass, `§7a.1` item 2 — the as-written clause above is kept visible and is NOT deleted): the TRUST half is now PINNED as four assertions, so this row can FAIL for the mutation it names:** **(1)** the applier **never calls `project`** during a call (a counting/instrumented double is not available, so the observable is structural: the applier's arguments are `(projection, sink)` and no `values`/`specOf` is reachable — a row asserts the module's exported signature and that the call is driven to completion **without any caller-supplied `values`/`specOf` in scope**, i.e. with a projection whose entries could not have come from any `project` call the row made); **(2)** **no re-derivation from a spec**: the row constructs a projection whose entries are **NOT what `project` would produce from the same shape** — e.g. a projection carrying a skip entry with a reason `project` would never emit for that shape (a `skipped` entry `{name:'--x', reason:'missing-value'}` for a key that IS present in the row's `values`) — and asserts the applier writes/records **the GIVEN skip unchanged**, never a recomputed one; **(3)** **the coercion is `F-10`'s and only `F-10`'s**: the row drives `{applied: {'--a': 12}}` and asserts `setProperty('--a', '12')` **and** that the caller's projection is **unmutated** (`I-14`, `F-15`) — *"exactly that record"* means **the same keys, the same order, the same skip entries**, with the value's string form produced fresh for the SINK; **(4)** a **non-record projection** is `M-11`'s no-op, not a re-validation path (§2.3 item 4's asymmetry). **A row asserting that the applier re-derives `malformed-spec`/`missing-value` for a hand-built projection FAILS this row** — that is the `F-10`-only exception, and re-derivation is not it. *(Note the register's `P-PJ-IM-6` statement cell already scopes its write-or-skip rule to a WELL-FORMED projection — the same boundary, `§5.5.1`.)* | `[T]` |
| **M-14** | **`applyVarsToRoot` is the same function** | `applyVarsToRoot(p, sink)` | identical behaviour **and identity** to `applyProjection` (a row asserts `applyVarsToRoot === applyProjection`) — the source-name alias is not a second implementation | `[T]` |
| **M-15** | **`ok` reflects the current call only** | one call with a skip, then a clean call | the second call: `ok === true`, `skipped` `[]` — nothing accumulates | `[T]` |
| **M-16** | **A value of `0` IS applied** | `{name:'--z', unit:'px'}`, value `0` | `applied` is `{'--z': '0'}` — **`0` is not treated as absent, not skipped, and not coerced** | `[T]` |
| **M-17** | **A very large and a very small finite value** | `1e21`, `-0`, `5e-324`, `Number.MAX_VALUE` | all finite ⇒ **applied** as `String(value) + unit` (`-0`'s string is `'0'`; `1e21`'s is `'1e+21'`); a row records the **exact** strings, so a later pass cannot "improve" the number formatting silently | `[T]` |
| **M-18** | **A caller-supplied name `'__proto__'` is an ORDINARY key (`A-3`)** | `project({k: 1}, {k: {name: '__proto__', unit: ''}})` | **own key `'__proto__'`** in `applied`, value `'1'`; `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(applied, '__proto__') === true`; `Object.keys(applied)` contains `'__proto__'` **in spec order**; **`Object.getPrototypeOf(applied) === null`** (the record's prototype was NOT mutated); `applied['__proto__']` reads back `'1'` as an own value, **not** the prototype; `skipped` is `[]`; `ok === true`; and with the sibling rows below, **`I-1`'s partition still holds** (`'__proto__'` appears in **no** `skipped` entry) | `[T]` |
| **M-19** | **The null-prototype record's own membership API (`A-3`)** | `project({k: 1}, {k: {name: '__proto__', unit: ''}})` | **`applied.hasOwnProperty` is `undefined`** (calling it throws `TypeError`) — the pinned membership test is **`Object.hasOwn(applied, name)`** / `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(applied, name)`; **`Object.getPrototypeOf(applied) === null`** for **every** returned `applied` record, including one with only ordinary names; **`Object.keys` / spread-into-a-null-prototype-target / `in` / `Object.entries` / `JSON.stringify` behave normally**; and **a deep-equality helper that compares prototypes is written with the null prototype in mind** (the row states which of the two forms it uses, so a mystery failure cannot hide a contract surprise) | `[T]` |
